Long-term        atmosphere–livestock grassland system (Calanca et al., projections are mainly provided to assess the impacts of cur-      2007). Five interacting sub-models of soil biology and rent management on soil organic carbon storage and GHG             physics, microclimate, vegetation, and grazing herbivores emissions.                                                         constitute the model structure. The model runs on a daily (or hourly) time step, and inputs require soil property data, management and meteorological characteristics (global so- 2.1   Models lar radiation, minimum and maximum air temperature, rel- ative humidity, wind speed, precipitation, and atmospheric The CERES-EGC model was used to simulate croplands                 CO$_2$ concentration). The soil is described in six sublayers, in Europe. CERES-EGC is a process-based biogeochemical             allowing us to parameterise different soil depths with site- model in the soil–plant–atmosphere domain adapted from             specific soil physical and chemical characteristics. Manage- CERES (Jones and Kiniry, 1986). The model is designed              ment includes grazing, mowing and N fertilisation. Grazing to simulate C and N dynamics; heat transfer; and water ex-         is considered a dairy or suckling system managed by graz- changes from soil, plants and the atmosphere. It works at a        ing periods with specific stocking density and live weight. daily time step designed to the field scale. Inputs require me-    Indoor periods are not simulated. Vegetation cover is con- teorological and management data as forcing variables and          sidered a homogeneous cover with a fixed legume fraction. soil and crop data as factors. Meteorological data are consti-     The vegetation cover comprises the root system and three tuted by daily minimum and maximum temperature, precip-            shoot compartments (laminae, sheaths and stems, and ears) itation, global solar radiation, and wind speed. Management        divided into age classes. Soil C dynamics (based on the CEN- includes tillage, irrigation, fertilisation, information on sow-   TURY model; Parton et al., 1994) are computed in five pools: ing and incorporation of crop residues. Soil is divided into       a structural and a metabolic pool for fresh organic carbon sublayers with specific depth, physical and chemical char-         (plant residues) and an active, a slow and a passive pool for acteristics. Simulated crop species include maize (grain and       the microbially processed organic carbon. M. Carozzi et al.: Effects of climate change in European croplands and grasslands                                                    3033 [SOURCE: Page_13]

Figure 6. The N$_2$ O emission factor (EF %) for croplands and grasslands in European administrative regions (NUTS$_2$). The EF is reported for the historical period (1985–2004) and the difference 1 from the middle (2030–2049) and the end (2080–2099) of the century for the two climatic scenarios RCP$_4$.5 and RCP$_8$.5. The EF is calculated as the ratio between the N emitted as N$_2$ O from croplands (irrigable scenario) and grasslands and the N introduced into the system as fertiliser (not including the N added by animal excretion, crop residue, atmospheric deposition, soil mineralisation and fixation). [SOURCE: Page_13]

Figure 7. The emission factor (EF) for N$_2$ O (%) for the different crops and grasslands for the historical period (1985–2004), towards the mid-century (2030–2049) and towards the end of the century (2080–2099), for the two climatic scenarios RCP$_4$.5 and RCP$_8$.5. The EF is the ratio between the N emitted as N$_2$ O from crops and grasslands and the N applied as fertiliser. [SOURCE: Page_13]

4     Discussion [SOURCE: Page_14]

4.1    Production [SOURCE: Page_14]

Results from this study confirmed that the effects of climate change, implying shifts in the temperature, precipitation and plant growing length among other factors, represent a serious drawback to plant production. Figure 11. Net ecosystem production (NEP) for croplands and grasslands in European administrative borders (NUTS$_2$). Results are reported for the historical period (1985–2004) and difference 1 from the middle (2030–2049) and the end (2080–2099) of the century for the two climatic scenarios RCP$_4$.5 and RCP$_8$.5. NEP for croplands is reported with the irrigable scenario (see the text). [SOURCE: Page_16]

Table 2. The net greenhouse gas exchange (NGHGE) and net greenhouse gas budget (NGB) in Europe during the historical and two climate change scenarios. The elements of the budget are reported: N$_2$ O, CH$_4$ and the net ecosystem production (NEP; for the sign convention, negative values represent a stock of carbon). Results are in Tg CO$_2$ eq. yr−1 . Between brackets is standard deviation. [SOURCE: Page_16]
